# What purlis decides about a chat today

Research for #6970, part of the wayfinder map #6969 ("One project policy governs everything a
chat can do"), under ruling Q307. This file lists every decision purlis makes about a chat today.
It is the candidate list of policy keys.

- **Source:** purlis `main` at `eb9c63fe8` (2026-11-16). All paths are repo-relative.
- **Read only:** nothing in the product was changed.
- **References:** every `file:line` below resolves to a file in the tree, at a line within that
  file: 2,038 references, all checked by script. A sample of about a dozen was read by hand
  against what its row claims.

## How to read the tables

Each row has these columns:

| Column | What it holds |
|---|---|
| **Decision** | What purlis decides, in plain words. |
| **Where** | The line that decides it. |
| **Default** | What happens today when nobody has changed anything. |
| **Who changes it** | Who can change it today. One or more of: **nobody** (hard-coded); the **project file** (`purlis.toml`/`charter.toml` and its `.local` twin, or another committed file, named); **the person** (a window setting, a CLI flag, a machine setting, or a consent button); an **admin lock** (a key of `/etc/purlis/policy.json`, named); a **grant file** (named). |
| **Harness** | Which harnesses it applies to: `all`, `claude` (Claude Code), `codex`, `opencode`, `acp`. "Harness-independent" means the decision is made in a `purlis` command or in the app, whatever the harness. |
| **Flags** | **HARD** means a hard-coded limit or outcome that nobody can change. **MULTI** means the same decision is made, or its constant is defined, in more than one place; the row names every place. |

Terms used throughout:

- **Sandbox levels:** "Full" is the full sandbox (`Level::Full`). "Floor" is the basic sandbox
  (`Level::Floor`), which is the default.
- **The wrap:** purlis's own Seatbelt/Landlock wrap around the whole harness. Codex and opencode
  run in it everywhere; Claude Code runs in it on Linux and on runners. Otherwise Claude Code
  uses its own sandbox, configured by settings purlis writes in
  `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/claude.rs:174`.
- **Sandboxable harnesses:** only Claude Code, Codex and opencode can be sandboxed
  (`crates/purlis-core/src/harness.rs:107`). Every other harness, ACP agents included, runs
  unsandboxed (`crates/purlis-core/src/acp.rs:13`).
- **Row numbers** restart in each table. Cite a row as `<family or table> #<n>`.

## What cuts across the families

### Who can change what today

1. **Two things are already called "policy".**
   - The team's `[sandbox] policy = local | never` in the project file.
   - The administrator's `/etc/purlis/policy.json`, whose 16 `sandbox.*` keys
     (`crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/policy.rs:838`) and its `dispatch.*` keys are the admin
     locks.

   The code merges the team's `never` into the admin lock's opt-out check, so one predicate
   answers both (Sandbox files and paths; Admin locks).
2. **The admin locks reach only the sandbox and dispatch.**
   - They are read from `/etc/purlis/policy.json` only, with no organisation layer and no Windows
     layer.
   - Nothing locks skills, MCP tools, plugins, model choice, memory or plane writes, pools,
     retention or push.
   - One key covers several things. `socket-grants` covers sockets, connect ports, listen ports
     and the certificate check together. `persona-tools` also turns off Run grants and login
     tools.
   - About 40 call sites across core and the app repeat the same lock check (Admin locks rows
     marked MULTI).
3. **"No built-in limits" (Q307 Q4) conflicts with a standing rule for guards.**
   - `crates/purlis-core/src/toolgate.rs:75` (`OVERRIDE_NOTE`, charter#370): *"There is
     deliberately no config key, environment variable or switch that lifts a purlis denial."*
   - Making guards policy keys reverses this. The spec has to record that, together with how the
     policy itself stays out of a chat's reach.
4. **Grants and limits are protected only by the sandbox.**
   - A chat in a project with the sandbox off can write the project file's `[dispatch.grants]`,
     `app/sandbox.json` and `app/dispatch-never.json` (Dispatch, Notes; D-1437-R2).
   - Q306's sealed grants are the first answer to this. Every grant store listed here needs the
     same treatment.
5. **The harness permission mode is not a purlis setting.**
   - Today it comes only from the flags in a profile's command and from the person's own harness
     settings.
   - purlis decides two cases:
     - A dispatched task starts in its asker's mode, capped at auto; bypass never travels.
     - A skill grade's chat is pinned to `default`/`plan`.
   - A policy key for the permission mode would be new.
6. **Almost everything is hard-coded.** 819 of 1,199 rows are HARD.
   - In the guards, the only things anyone can change are:
     - `[plane] refuse_unsandboxed_commit`;
     - the scan allowlist (`.purlis-scan-allow.toml`);
     - the project's list of forge hosts;
     - attendance, which decides whether the release floor applies.

### Decisions made in more than one place

247 rows are MULTI. The patterns that matter most for one engine:

- **Core and the app decide the same thing.**
  - The opt-out refusal for runners is in both `crates/purlis-core/src/start.rs` and
    `app/src-tauri/src/hostedsessions.rs`.
  - A skill grade's chat time is 5 minutes in `crates/purlis-core/src/skills/grades.rs:53` and
    again in `app/src-tauri/src/skillgrade.rs:56`.
  - The lock checks above repeat across core and the app.
- **Per harness, one decision behaves differently.**
  - A host listed without a port reaches any port on Claude Code, but only 443 and 80 on the
    wrapped harnesses.
  - The floor's process denies (`launchctl`, `osascript`, `open`, cron, Apple Events) exist only
    in the wrap. Nothing equivalent was found for Claude Code at the floor.
  - Only Claude Code gets the pre-allows that keep the harness from asking:
    - for dispatch and handoff tools (Dispatch #97-104);
    - for the browser tools.

    Other harnesses still prompt beside purlis's own grant.
  - Codex gets its approval policy from purlis only when sandboxed
    (`crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/codex.rs:774`). An unsandboxed Codex chat keeps Codex's own
    settings (`crates/purlis-core/src/harness/codex.rs:211`).
  - Codex has no file-read guard for vault files.
  - opencode has no sub-agent detection, and ACP is not named in dispatch at all.
- **Two layers apply the same rule.**
  - The git hooks and the commit/push diff scan read the same hooks from two sides (Guards: git
    #16-20 and credential D1-D10).
  - The floor guard is restated by the release, merge and forge tables. The floor guard table is
    the canonical one.

### Holes that a policy key would have to fill (no decision today)

- **Workspaces and files**
  - Cross-workspace reads are open at every sandbox level; only the denial classes are
    read-denied.
  - Writes follow a chat's working directory, so a chat at the plane root writes every
    workspace.
- **Resources and cost**
  - Desktop chats have no process, CPU or memory limit; only runner containers are bounded.
  - There is no token, cost or turn cap; purlis only reads the vendor's limit.
- **Git and the forge**
  - There is no list of protected branches. The only rule refuses a push to the remote's default
    branch (`crates/purlis-core/src/pieceupdate.rs:1854`).
  - There is no allowlist of forge verbs. With the person's own `gh`/`glab` login, a chat can
    close issues, add labels, edit issues and create pull requests.
- **Skills and remote chats**
  - Skill owners are not enforced. The code waits on "the policy layer" (SD-14, #697).
  - Remote chats are only a state kind, with no allow or deny.
- **Harnesses outside the sandbox**
  - Harnesses that cannot be sandboxed (ACP and others) have the person's whole filesystem and
    network.
  - Under Q307 Q5 such a start would be refused unless the policy allows it.
- **Grants that do not last**
  - These decisions live only in memory and are lost on restart:
    - Allow next time, Keep blocked and Allow for this chat;
    - the browser posture;
    - fill and login grants.

### Limits that bound nothing a chat may do

- Five `[dispatch]` "limits" only time a quiet-task note or the "Stuck?" prompt (Dispatch
  #56-57).
- Some fail-closed bounds refuse the chat when hit. Loosening them weakens the sandbox rather than
  granting anything, so they need a ruling on whether they are keys at all. They are:
  - `KeptUntold`, `Unread` and `RepositoriesUnfound`;
  - the 1 MiB cap on `charter.toml`;
  - the 768 KiB cap on a profile.

### Where the code and its docs disagree

- **Codex approval policy.**
  - `crates/purlis-core/src/attendance.rs:22` says purlis sets Codex's approval policy.
  - In fact it does so only for a sandboxed Codex chat (see above).
- **Expiring asks.**
  - ADR 0121's 2026-11-10 table says no expiring ask is held open.
  - `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/asks.rs:59` (`MOST_HELD_OPEN`, 15 minutes) holds connections
    open while the person is Quiet.
- **Signing of the app's commits.**
  - `crates/purlis-core/src/gitbroker.rs:1272` says a commit the app makes for a chat is never
    signed.
  - It is signed now (`commitsign`).
- **Engine refusal.** Nothing sets `EngineSettings::run_outdated`, so the 21-day refusal of an
  outdated browser engine is in effect hard-coded.
- **Old Claude Code.** A Claude Code older than 2.1.285 bypasses purlis's proxy, its decisions
  and its network record.

### Not verified

- Whether ACP harnesses reach `guard pretooluse` at all.
- Whether the merge hold's second press is enforced only in the window.
- Whether both brokered commit paths check the `[sandbox]`/`[chat_env]`/`[dispatch]` rule.
- Which harnesses besides Claude Code run the persona tool gate.
- Whether Claude Code's macOS listen grants are consistent.
  - `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox.rs:7466` says no door opens for a Claude Code chat.
  - `app/src-tauri/src/hooks.rs:2120` describes a dial-in door.
- The browser rows were gathered by a second-level reader. Their references pass the existence
  check above, but fewer of them were read by hand.

| N1 | Whether a chat's network is held at all (a sandbox is in force: project `[sandbox]` turned on, or a policy that requires it) | `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox.rs:517` (`Plane::in_force`), `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox.rs:785` (`Said::in_force`) | Not held unless the project has `[sandbox]` with a mode, or policy forbids opt-out | project file (`charter.toml [sandbox] mode`); admin lock (`opt-out: false`); the person (level choice, "Start without the sandbox") | claude, codex, opencode | Belongs to the Sandbox family too; listed because no other row applies without it |
| N2 | At the Basic sandbox (the floor) a chat reaches **every** host through its own proxy, with nothing asked, except this machine's own addresses and policy's `never-hosts` | `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox.rs:4355` (`reach::Reach::anything()`), `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/reach.rs:242`; Claude settings `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/claude.rs:529` (`floor`, no `allowedDomains`) | Floor = open web | the person's or team's level choice (Sandbox family); admin lock `never-hosts` | claude, codex, opencode | MULTI (core reach + Claude settings writer) |

## Guards

Row numbers restart per table; cite a row as `<table> #<n>`. Two overlaps are kept on purpose, because each table reads the same code from a different side. Dedupe them when building keys:
- **Floor guard vs release/merge/forge.** The floor guard table (`floorguard.rs`, `floorguard/{merge,token}.rs`) is the canonical one. The release table (#1-11), merge #1 and #6-10, and forge #21 restate the same lines from the forge side. They label the harness "hooked harnesses" where the floor table says "all". "Hooked harnesses" is the safer reading: ACP's route into `guard pretooluse` is unconfirmed.
- **Git hooks vs diff scan.** In the git table, git hooks #16-20 give each hook's outcome. Credential's diff scan D1-D10 gives the scanner's limits and lists for the same hooks (`githook.rs`, `diffscan.rs`).
- **guardcaps.rs.** Its full rows are in the plumbing table. The tool gate table and floor guard #15-16 only point to it.

### git

Paths are repo-relative. "Bash guard" = the `PreToolUse` shell guard (`toolgate::verdict`, run by `purlis guard` for every harness whose shell tool reaches purlis's hook). "Brokered" = work a chat hands the app over its hook socket (`hookwire::Ask::Git` / `Ask::Commit`), run by the app outside the sandbox.

### credential

Paths are repo-relative. "Bash guard" = `purlis hook pretooluse` (`crates/purlis-cli/src/guard.rs:140` -> `crates/purlis-core/src/toolgate.rs:396 verdict`), wired for claude (`hookreg.rs:90`, matcher `Bash`), codex (`plugin.rs:161 CODEX`) and opencode (`opencode.rs:139`). "Read guard" = `pretooluse-read` (`hookreg.rs:96`, matcher `Read|Grep`), wired for claude and opencode (`opencode.rs:145,151`), not codex (`plugin.rs:127-134`). "Chat git hooks" = `purlis git-hook <name>` (`crates/purlis-cli/src/githook.rs`), armed in a chat's clones only (operator's own terminal unarmed, `scanallow.rs:19-20`).

### save

Plane save is `planegit::save_as` (window button, `purlis save`, auto-save, launch, live switch, rename, skill change); repo save is `reposave::save_as`; settings come from `planesave::Settings` (`charter.toml` = Shared, `charter.local.toml` = Local, Local overrides key by key, only while git ignores the Local file).

### shell guard

The shell guard does not block anything. It stands in front of a harness that the person starts by hand in a plain shell tab (ADR 0062). `shellseg` is the parser that every Bash refusal relies on.

| # | Decision | Where | Default | Who changes it | Harness | Flags |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which harnesses get a shim in a shell tab | `crates/purlis-core/src/shellguard.rs:56` (`SHIMMED`); mirrors `crates/purlis-core/src/harness.rs:107` (`Harness::ALL`) | claude, codex, opencode | nobody | claude / codex / opencode (no ACP agents) | **MULTI** (two separate constants that must stay in step); **HARD** |
| 2 | A harness started by hand is never blocked: purlis prints a warning, tells the app, then `exec`s the real program | `crates/purlis-core/src/shellguard.rs:383` (`plan`); `crates/purlis-cli/src/shellguard.rs:18` (`run`) | allow, plus a warning | nobody | claude / codex / opencode | **MULTI** (the core decides, the CLI acts); **HARD** (no setting) |
| 3 | The app is told (banner with "Open as chat") only when the hook socket and a chat number above 0 are both in the environment | `crates/purlis-core/src/shellguard.rs:391` | told inside an app's shell tab, otherwise silent | nobody | claude / codex / opencode | HARD |
| 4 | After the person's own rc files run, the shim directory goes first on `PATH` again, for zsh (`ZDOTDIR`) and bash (`--rcfile`) only. Other shells get `PATH` alone, so their rc files can put a harness ahead of the shim | `crates/purlis-core/src/shellguard.rs:194` | zsh and bash covered; other shells best effort | nobody | harness-independent | HARD |
| 5 | The shim directory is owner-only (0700) | `crates/purlis-core/src/shellguard.rs:123` | 0700 | nobody | harness-independent | HARD |
| 6 | No shims where they cannot be written (non-unix, or a write error), so the shell tab is a plain shell with no warning | `app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:297` (`shell_tab_shims`) | shims on unix | nobody | harness-independent | HARD |
| 7 | No real program on `PATH` outside the shims gives exit 127; a failed `exec` gives 126 | `crates/purlis-cli/src/shellguard.rs:36`, `:56` | 127 / 126 | nobody | harness-independent | HARD |
| 8 | How a Bash command line is split into commands: punctuation `();<>\|&` plus newline separate commands; whitespace is ` \t\r` | `crates/purlis-core/src/shellseg.rs:77`, `:80`, `:137` | CPython `shlex` semantics (recorded oracle) | nobody (changes only by re-recording the oracle, ADR 0046) | all harnesses that run the Bash guard | HARD |
| 9 | A line that will not lex falls back to a cruder segmentation and is flagged as "not a parse", so callers also match the raw string (fail closed) | `crates/purlis-core/src/shellseg.rs:1481` (`read`), `:1410` (`fallback_segments`) | fail closed | nobody | all (Bash guard) | HARD |

### tool gate

This is the `PreToolUse` Bash guard (`toolgate::verdict`). Each row is one rule ("arm"). Arms owned by other families (A leak, A2 credential, A8 hook skip, A9b branch git, merge hold) appear here only with their place in the order. Their contents are in those families' sections.

| # | Decision | Where | Default | Who changes it | Harness | Flags |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Every Bash call goes through the guard before it runs: matcher `Bash`, 10 s hook timeout | `crates/purlis-core/src/hookreg.rs:90` (timeout `:93`); Codex config `crates/purlis-core/src/plugin_install.rs:1229`, `:1234`; opencode `crates/purlis-core/src/opencode.rs:137` | on, 10 s | nobody | claude (hooks.json), codex (config.toml), opencode (plugin) | **MULTI** (written once per harness); **HARD** |
| 2 | On opencode, every tool without its own hook word (MCP tools included) is routed to the Bash guard; a tool with no `command` is allowed | `crates/purlis-core/src/opencode.rs:195` (`DEFAULT_PRE`) | the Bash guard | nobody | opencode | HARD |
| 3 | Fail closed: an unknown `pretooluse*`/`posttooluse*` hook word answers block (exit 2), and a crash in a PreToolUse hook refuses the call | `crates/purlis-cli/src/main.rs:2266` (`is_a_tool_hook`), `:2290` (`BLOCK = 2`); `crates/purlis-cli/src/guard.rs:454` (`refuse_on_a_crash`) | block | nobody | all | **MULTI**; **HARD** |
| 4 | No override exists: no config key, environment variable or switch lifts a denial; the only way past is the person running the command themselves | `crates/purlis-core/src/toolgate.rs:75` (`OVERRIDE_NOTE`) | no override | nobody | all | **HARD** (by design) |
| 5 | Retry-shaped arms (rider, forge/charter substitution, dispatch text, hook skip, handoff brief, branch git) name the shape to retry and drop the override note | `crates/purlis-core/src/toolgate.rs:92` (`RETRY_SHAPED`) | 7 reasons | nobody | all | HARD |
| 6 | The arm order is fixed (first refusal wins): caps, A, A2, A3, A3b, A3c, A3d, A4, A5, A6, A7, A7d, A7e, A7b, A7c, A8, A9, A9b, A10 | `crates/purlis-core/src/toolgate.rs:236` (`arms!`), `:424` (`fn arms`) | as listed | nobody | all | HARD |

### prose guard

### floor guard

Arm A4, the release floor: a chat nobody is watching may not publish, merge or print a credential. It denies rather than asks, and an attended chat passes.

| # | Decision | Where | Default | Who changes it | Harness | Flags |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | An unattended chat is denied a publish (deny, never ask); an attended chat is not checked | `crates/purlis-core/src/floorguard.rs:161` (`release_floor_reason`), `:272` (`if !unattended`) | deny when unattended | the person, by attending the chat (attendance fact) or by the harness permission mode | all (in a plane) | HARD (the verbs) |
| 2 | How attendance is read. A chat the app started uses the app's attendance fact; on Claude Code only, a line's `bypassPermissions` lowers it to unattended; on Codex the fact alone counts (Codex reports bypass on every line); a harness the app did not start uses `permission_mode == bypassPermissions` only | `crates/purlis-core/src/attendance.rs:181` (`of_a_line`), `:158` (`PROMPTS_OFF`); `crates/purlis-core/src/toolgate.rs:369`; `crates/purlis-core/src/harness/claude.rs:409` | as described | the person (attending, permission mode) | claude / codex / opencode differ | **MULTI** (attendance is also read by the commit gate and the briefing) |
| 3 | If the app does not answer the attendance ask within 500 ms (or answers anything but attended), the chat counts as unattended | `crates/purlis-cli/src/guard.rs:175` (`ATTENDANCE_IS_ANSWERED_WITHIN`), `:199` | unattended | nobody | all (app-started chats) | **HARD** |
| 4 | Forge publish verbs refused: gh release create, gh pr merge, glab release create, glab mr merge, `purlis change land`; `gh pr create` is deliberately absent | `crates/purlis-core/src/floorguard.rs:78` (`PUBLISH_FORGE`) | 5 rows | nobody | all | **HARD** |
| 5 | `purlis skill publish` is refused unattended | `crates/purlis-core/src/floorguard.rs:89` (`PUBLISH_SINCE`) | deny | nobody | all | HARD |
| 6 | Verb aliases count as the same verb: `glab mr accept`, `gh release new` | `crates/purlis-core/src/floorguard/merge.rs:68` (`VERB_ALIASES`) | — | nobody | all | HARD |
| 7 | Read beyond the table: `gh`/`glab` api, alias, auth, config get, and `glab mr create/new` (auto-merge) | `crates/purlis-core/src/floorguard/merge.rs:84` (`HELD_BEYOND_THE_TABLE`) | checked | nobody | all | HARD |
| 8 | `git tag <name>` (creating a tag) is refused unattended; the read-only/local flags in `TAG_HARMLESS` (-l, -d, --contains, -v …) are allowed | `crates/purlis-core/src/floorguard.rs:336`; `:106` (`TAG_HARMLESS`, 11) | deny creation | nobody | all | HARD |
| 9 | `git push` with `--tags`/`--follow-tags`, a `refs/tags/` ref, or a version-shaped word (`v?\d+.\d+…`) is refused unattended | `crates/purlis-core/src/floorguard.rs:352`; regex `:145` | deny | nobody | all | HARD |
| 10 | Auto-merge by other routes: a push option that speaks of merging, `git config push.pushOption`/`alias.*` that would publish, `GIT_CONFIG_*` env carrying pushoption | `crates/purlis-core/src/floorguard/merge.rs:655`, `:788`, `:816`; `:785` (`HELD_GIT` push, tag) | deny | nobody | all | HARD |
| 11 | Raw `gh api`/`glab api` writes to release, tag, ref or merge endpoints, GraphQL merge mutations, and unreadable methods or endpoints | `crates/purlis-core/src/floorguard/merge.rs:447` (`api_reason`), `:507`, `:544` | deny | nobody | all | HARD |
| 12 | Printing the forge token is refused unattended: `gh`/`glab auth token`, `auth git-credential`, `auth docker-helper`, `auth status --show-token`/`-t`, and `config get` of a key containing "token" | `crates/purlis-core/src/floorguard/token.rs:61` | deny | nobody | all | HARD |
| 13 | `git credential fill`, and a credential helper asked to `get`, are refused unattended | `crates/purlis-core/src/floorguard/token.rs:76` | deny | nobody | all | HARD |

| 15 | Work budget: the floor reads at most 6× the line's length + 64 KiB across all nesting levels, or the line is refused as unreadable | `crates/purlis-core/src/floorguard.rs:229` (`WORK_FACTOR`), `:233` (`WORK_SLACK`) | 6×, 64 KiB | nobody | all | **HARD** |

### handoff guard

Arm A7, gated by the plane. Consent to a handoff itself is the app's dispatch grant and is not decided here.

| # | Decision | Where | Default | Who changes it | Harness | Flags |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A helper sub-agent may not run `purlis handoff`; "sub-agent" means a non-empty `agent_id` on a harness where it was measured | `crates/purlis-core/src/handoffguard.rs:647` (`from_a_subagent`), `:88` (`MEASURED_SUBAGENT_HARNESSES` claude-code, codex) | deny | nobody | claude, codex (never opencode) | **MULTI** (same rule as A7d, `dispatchguard.rs:190`); HARD |
| 2 | A handoff inside a string or heredoc that a shell runs (`eval`, `sh -c`, fed to a shell, `find -exec`) is refused | `crates/purlis-core/src/handoffguard.rs:348` (`shell_string_handoff`), `:157` (`REASON_SHELL_STRING`) | deny | nobody | all (in a plane) | HARD |
| 3 | A handoff whose words are reached only through an expansion, glob or substitution (`SPELLING_MARKS`), or that is not at the start of its own command, is refused | `crates/purlis-core/src/handoffguard.rs:385` (`handoff_segment`), `:93` (`SPELLING_MARKS`) | deny | nobody | all (in a plane) | HARD |
| 4 | The brief must come from exactly one quoted heredoc in the same call. Refused: here-string, `<` file, pipe, no heredoc, more than one heredoc, xargs/parallel, `<&`, an unquoted heredoc, a live substitution anywhere in the call | `crates/purlis-core/src/handoffguard.rs:1018` (`brief_source`); `:100` (`REDIRECT_READS`), `:970` (`STDIN_TO_WORDS`) | deny | nobody | all (in a plane) | HARD |
| 5 | A report-back handoff (`--report`) needs no heredoc; only a live substitution in it is refused | `crates/purlis-core/src/handoffguard.rs:783` | deny only on substitution | nobody | all (in a plane) | HARD |
| 6 | `purlis handoff --help`/`-h` with nothing fed in, and only one handoff on the line, passes | `crates/purlis-core/src/handoffguard.rs:823` (`asks_for_help`) | allow | nobody | all | HARD |
| 7 | A handoff in a `case` branch, a function body, or nested deeper than the first level is refused; past the nesting cap it is `too-big-to-check` | `crates/purlis-core/src/handoffguard.rs:569` (`placed_in`), `:707` | deny | nobody | all (in a plane) | **MULTI** (walk shared with consentspelling and rulespelling); HARD |
| 8 | An unattended (`bypassPermissions`) handoff is no longer refused here; the app decides it under the dispatch grant | `crates/purlis-core/src/handoffguard.rs:696` (doc); see `dispatchunattended` | allow (app decides) | grant file / the person (dispatch grant) | all | — |

## Dispatch

"Manifest" = the project's committed `purlis.toml` / `charter.toml`; "local" = `purlis.local.toml` / `charter.local.toml`; "policy" = `/etc/purlis/policy.json` (`crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/policy.rs:160`).

### Who may dispatch at all, and in what order it is judged

| # | Decision | Where | Default | Who changes it | Harness | Flags |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Order of checks for every dispatch/handoff: helper → held → persona exists/not draft → policy → profile → limits → grant → memory | `crates/purlis-core/src/dispatchdecision.rs:608` (`decide`) | as listed | nobody | harness-independent (app decision) | HARD (order fixed) |
| 2 | A harness's helper sub-agent may not dispatch (Bash `purlis dispatch`, any `dispatch_*` MCP tool) | `crates/purlis-core/src/dispatchguard.rs:190` (`refusal`), `:236` (`tool_refusal`); decision `crates/purlis-core/src/dispatchdecision.rs:613`; sub-agent detection `crates/purlis-core/src/handoffguard.rs:647` | refused | nobody | claude, codex only (`MEASURED_SUBAGENT_HARNESSES`, `crates/purlis-core/src/handoffguard.rs:88`); opencode/acp: never refused | MULTI (hook + decision); HARD |
| 3 | A helper sub-agent may not run `purlis worktree add/commit/push` (task work) | `crates/purlis-core/src/dispatchguard.rs:106` (`A_TASKS_WORK`), `:190` | refused | nobody | claude, codex | HARD |

### Limits (`[dispatch]` table)

All read by `crates/purlis-core/src/dispatchlimits.rs:571` (`in_force`) and judged by `:903` (`decide`). Levels: manifest `[dispatch]` → `[dispatch.workspaces.<ws>]` → `[dispatch.personas.<p>]` (most specific wins); local `[dispatch]` may only lower; policy `dispatch.<key>` is a ceiling. Defaults at `crates/purlis-core/src/dispatchlimits.rs:267` (`when_unset`). Settings view `app/src-tauri/src/dispatchlimits.rs`.

| # | Decision | Where | Default | Who changes it | Harness | Flags |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 47 | `running-per-chat`: tasks one chat may have running | `crates/purlis-core/src/dispatchlimits.rs:269`, `:930` | 6 | project file `[dispatch]` (ws/persona levels); person (local, lower only); admin ceiling | all | |
| 48 | `live-per-lineage`: live chats under one person-started chat | `:270`, `:932` | 16 | same | all | |
| 49 | `depth`: dispatch chain depth | `:271`, `:920` | 3 | same | all | |
| 50 | `depth` never above 8, at any level incl. policy | `:114` (`DEEPEST`), `:318` | 8 | nobody | all | HARD |
| 51 | `messages-per-minute` per pair of chats (both ways); 0 stops messages only | `:272`, `:1051` (`may_send`); `crates/purlis-core/src/dispatchtalk.rs:202` | 10 | same as #47 | all | MULTI |
| 52 | `may-dispatch`: running tasks across all chats of the asking persona (persona table only) | `:283`, `:939` | no cap | project file `[dispatch.personas.<p>]`; admin ceiling | all | |
| 53 | `may-run-at-once`: chats running as target persona at once | `:283`, `:948` | no cap | same | all | |

## Vaults and secrets

Paths are relative to the repo root. `crates/purlis-core/src/` = `crates/purlis-core/src/`, `app/src-tauri/src/` = `app/src-tauri/src/`. Every line number was checked with `grep -n`. The machine grant file is `<config>/purlis/sandbox-grants/<clone-key>.json` (`crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/local.rs:41`, `:194`).

**Notes**

- Policy lock keys in this family come from `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/policy.rs:843-851` (the list) and `:985-993` (parsing). `allow-scopes` and `proposed-changes` also gate Run grants and fill grants (rows 36, 37, 58), but they belong to the sandbox family.
- The vault-grant check (`forbids_vault_grants` plus `granted_vaults`) is copied in at least 10 places (crates/purlis-core/src/secrets/brokered.rs ×6, app/src-tauri/src/vaultroute.rs, app/src-tauri/src/vaults.rs, app/src-tauri/src/secretasks.rs, app/src-tauri/src/asking.rs ×2). It is the strongest candidate for a single policy rule.
- Command-wait timeouts are a hard-coded table per harness (`crates/purlis-core/src/secrets/ask.rs:516`). It has only `ClaudeCode | Opencode | Codex`. ACP and other harnesses fall to "unknown": 10 s base for asks, 180 s for the exec deadline. These two "unknown" defaults disagree with each other.
- I found no explicit timeout for a pending browser secret fill. It ends when the call goes (`Heard::Gone`, app/src-tauri/src/secretfills.rs:662), so the MCP client's call timeout sets it.
- The window-only rule for answering asks and Run grants is checked in core (`HumanScope::LocalUi`, ask.rs:1319, run.rs:981/1019/1275) and also enforced by Tauri commands not being served on the link (`WINDOW_ONLY`, per app/src-tauri/src/secretasks.rs docs). That is two layers, not two decisions.
- policy.rs:107-110 says `persona-tools: false` "has the same effect" on Run grants. The code agrees: `refuses_run_grant` falls through to `refuses_tool` (policy.rs:772-781).
- Credentialed MCP servers and `disallowed-tools` reach Claude Code only. On Codex and opencode the servers are not started (chatstart.rs docs), so persona vault access by MCP differs by harness.
- `secretshape.rs` is covered only as the vault-reference exemption (row 84). Its credential-shape masking belongs to the redaction/memory family.

## The browser

Paths are repo-relative. "Admin lock" means `/etc/purlis/policy.json` (`crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/policy.rs:160`); its keys are `sandbox.allow-scopes` (`scope_removed`, policy.rs:633), `sandbox.never-hosts` (`refuses_grant`, policy.rs:592), `sandbox.proposed-changes` (`proposed_changes_refused`, policy.rs:749) and `sandbox.vault-grants` (`vault_grants_refused`, policy.rs:790). The browser tools are served by purlis's own MCP server on every harness, so "all" here means every harness that is given that server.

| # | Decision | Where | Default | Who changes it | Harness | Flags |
|---|----------|-------|---------|----------------|---------|-------|
| B1 | Whether a chat may have a browser at all: its first browser call asks the person once (This project's sites / Also the open web / Keep off). Nothing starts until they answer, and every call made meanwhile is refused | `app/src-tauri/src/chatbrowsers.rs:3396` (`answer_now`, Posture::Unasked→Asked); `Posture` at :206 | Ask on the first call; kept in memory only, so a relaunch asks again | the person (posture Ask buttons, `allow_chat_browser` :5322, `keep_chat_browser_off` :5527) | all | |
| B2 | Keep off refuses every browser call the chat makes, and tells it not to retry | `app/src-tauri/src/chatbrowsers.rs:3404` (`KEPT_OFF` :987) | n/a | the person (Keep off) | all | |
| B3 | "Always for this persona" answers the browser Ask for every chat of that persona in this project on this machine (project sites, or the open web too) | `app/src-tauri/src/chatbrowsers.rs:3390` (applies it), :5401 `allowed_always`; kept by `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/local.rs:1861` | none kept | the person (Always on the posture Ask); admin lock `allow-scopes` without `you` turns it off (`local.rs:1848` `browser_always_here`) and hides the offer (`app/src-tauri/src/asking.rs:2412`) | all | MULTI (offer in asking.rs, check in chatbrowsers.rs, read in local.rs) |

**Notes**

- No admin lock covers skills specifically; the only lock that bites is `sandbox.proposed-changes = "off"` (sandbox/policy.rs:749), which also refuses drafts (#21, #24). `[skill_policy]` is a committed project file, not a lock, and only governs source installs/updates/removals, not hand-made or chat-drafted skills.
- `[skill_policy]` with no `min_age_days` still turns a 7-day minimum age on: merely adding the table (e.g. only a block list) makes undated extension revisions uninstallable (#31/#32).
- Skill owners are informational; lifecycle.rs says a policy-gated review for changes to owned skills waits on "the policy layer" (SD-14, #697) — a direct candidate key.
- Grade `CHAT_TIME` is defined twice (core grades.rs:53 and app skillgrade.rs:56) with the same value; they can drift.
- `reach_chats` (#1) is evaluated per process (window, host, MCP server, sandbox) from the machine flag; a runner always holds skill folders (#18) regardless of flag.
- The plugin-pin decisions (`purlis@inline` always on, `charter@charter` off; harness_plugin.rs:13-20) and MCP pre-allow on non-Claude harnesses are adjacent but belong to the Plugins/MCP families.
- Line ranges for WINDOW_ONLY skill entries (ui.rs ~782-930) are approximate within the array starting at :203; the deciding line is :203.

## Admin locks

All locks are read from one file, `/etc/purlis/policy.json`, by `Locks::of` / `Locks::of_this_machine` in `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/policy.rs` (no other C9 layer is read: no MDM, no Windows key, no org server). "Who changes it" is always **admin lock** (the machine's root-owned file); a lock only narrows, never widens. Sandbox locks apply to every harness chat purlis confines (claude, codex, opencode, ACP agents); dispatch locks to every chat that dispatches.

| # | Decision | Where | Default | Who changes it | Harness | Flags |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1 | Where policy is read from: one fixed path, nothing in a project or the environment moves it | crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/policy.rs:160 (`MACHINE_FILE`), :267 (`Locks::of`), :317 (`of_this_machine`) | `/etc/purlis/policy.json` | nobody | all | **HARD** (path fixed so no chat can point it at its own file) |
| L2 | Policy file trusted only if regular file, not a link, root-owned, in a root-owned folder, not group/other-writable; ACLs not read | crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/policy.rs:1119 (`judge`), :1140 (`judge_folder`), :1160 (`read_trusted`, unix) | as stated | nobody | all | **HARD** |
| L3 | Policy file at most 64 KiB, `owner` clipped to 120 chars, refusal sentence to 240 | crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/policy.rs:163, :166, :169 | 64 KiB / 120 / 240 | nobody | all | **HARD** |
| L4 | No policy file read off Unix (Windows: no policy at all) | crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/policy.rs:1216 (`#[cfg(not(unix))] read_trusted` returns None) | none on Windows | nobody | all | **HARD** |
| L5 | A refused file (untrusted, not JSON, unknown key, bad value) refuses closed: every lock set except `presets`; dispatch off | crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/policy.rs:344 (`refused`); unknown keys :869, :889, :838 (`KEYS`, 16 sandbox keys), :1041 (`DISPATCH_LOCKS`); crates/purlis-core/src/dispatchlimits.rs:1385 (`ceiling_when_refused`) | n/a | nobody (behaviour); admin fixes file | all | **HARD** (fail-closed rule) |
| L6 | App re-reads the policy every 5 s and re-lists chats running under an older sandbox | app/src-tauri/src/sandboxing.rs:3634 (`POLICY_READ_EVERY`), :3670 (`watch_policy`) | 5 s | nobody | all | **HARD** |

Notes (admin locks):
- CONTEXT.md "Policy" says locks are "kept on this machine or for the organisation"; code reads only the machine file (policy.rs module doc, D-1343-1). No org layer, no Windows layer.
- The lock vocabulary is narrow: only `sandbox.*` (16 keys) and `dispatch.*`. Nothing locks skills (`[skill_policy]` is a committed project table, not an admin lock), MCP tools, plugins/extensions, model choice, memory/plane writes, pools/runners, retention or worktree push. Those are gaps for the unified policy.
- One key often governs several unrelated grants: `socket-grants` covers sockets, services, loopback ports, listening servers AND certificate checks; `persona-tools` also kills Run grants and login tools. A unified policy would want these split.
- Lock checks are duplicated across core and app (≈40 call sites; app hooks.rs, asking.rs, loginasks.rs, secretasks.rs, sandboxing.rs re-ask the same Locks). Any refactor should funnel through one decision function.
- Three hard-coded "lock bundles" (L28-L30) are built by overriding `Locks` fields in a thread-local, i.e. the lock type doubles as a per-chat restriction mechanism for grader and pool-help chats.
- Decided but not written: last-known-good keychain copy of the policy (ADR 0124 decision 15, plane-format.md).

**Notes**

- **Unattended-logins record is documented but not built.** `docs/plane-format.md:1147` describes `.charter/unattended-logins.json` (ADR 0087 §3, "decided, not yet written"). Nothing in `crates/` or `app/src-tauri/src/` reads or writes it.
- **Model choice is not a purlis decision yet.** Outside skill grades (`crates/purlis-core/src/skills/grades.rs:2200`, skills scope), purlis never picks a chat's model. It comes from the profile's command line or from the harness default (ADR 0087 D-2036-6). A sandboxed Claude chat also inherits `model` from the project's committed `.claude/settings.json` (row 18), so a committed file can set the model.
- **MCP `memory_add`/`todo_add` skip the brokered-write limits.** They are written by the MCP server itself (`chattools.rs:1476`, `:1565`), not brokered. So the 12 KiB cap and the 60-writes-a-minute rate (rows 30–31) do not apply to them. Only `persona_remember`, `workspace_vision`, `workspace_section` and `session_record` go through the app.
- **A chat can write `charter.toml` directly without the Settings-save refusals.** The refusals in rows 5–11 apply only to the person's Settings save. When a chat writes `charter.toml` itself (where its sandbox allows), only read-time ignoring applies. Which settings files the sandbox denies a chat is the sandbox section's to list (ADR 0067 §5: `charter.local.toml`, `harnesses/`, approval records).
- **Extension install has no in-chat refusal in the CLI.** `crates/purlis-cli/src/extensions.rs` has none. What keeps a chat out is the machine config directory's sandbox denial. Same for the extension record.
- **The trust records protect only under the sandbox.** `profiletrust.rs:12-19` says so itself: with the sandbox off, a chat can forge the profile/declaration approval records.
- **ACP and Codex get no pre-allow list for read/own-scope tools.** Rows 52–53 are Claude-only. Codex gets only the dispatch tools auto-approved, and opencode the same. ACP agents get the server but no purlis-side approval rules, so the agent's own prompts apply.
- **Proposals only work for sandboxed Claude chats via the hook.** Row 23 requires `chat_is_sandboxed_in` and Claude Code. An unsandboxed Claude chat writes those files directly (no proposal), and other harnesses must run `purlis propose`.
- **Overlap with other sections.** Rows 27 (state-dir edit deny), 29 (PLANTED names) and 59 (persona tool gate) touch guards, sandbox and asks. Rows 12 and 25 reference the admin locks (`sandbox.proposed-changes`). Keep one owner when merging.

### Other (B)

Pools, runners and their limits; runner taint, hand-overs and kill switch; relayed and hosted chats; retention; usage limits; branch folders (worktrees), commit, push, merge, update, remove; cross-repo change and landing; piece claims; protected branches.

## Notes from each reading

### Sandbox files and paths, processes

- **Cross-workspace reads are open at every level.** Only the denial classes are ever read-denied (`crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/grant.rs:37`, "No read is granted"). A chat in workspace A can read workspace B's files, other projects' files and the person's home (except vaults, keychains, purlis's records and other harnesses' sign-ins). What a chat writes is decided by its cwd alone (`crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox.rs:5248`), so a chat at the plane root writes every workspace at the full sandbox. A policy key for "which workspaces a chat reads or writes" has no code behind it today.
- **Two policies already exist under the name "policy".** One is the team's `[sandbox] policy = local|never` (`team.rs`). The other is the admin `/etc/purlis/policy.json` (`policy.rs`, 16 sandbox keys plus `dispatch`). `Locks` folds the team's `never` into `forbids_opt_out` (`policy.rs:493`), so one predicate answers both. The admin file is trusted only if root-owned and not group- or world-writable (`policy.rs:1119`), it is read on Unix only (Windows has none, `policy.rs:1213`), and it is capped at 64 KiB.
- **Level stores are split three ways.** The project says `mode` and `policy` in the committed `charter.toml`. The person says `level` and `personas` in `purlis.local.toml`. The confirmed record is in the config home (`local.rs` `levels_mine`). Any single policy must keep the rule that a chat-writable file's lower level is not honoured until it is confirmed (`personal.rs:25-32`).
- **The opt-out is refused in two places.** Runners refuse it in `crates/purlis-core/src/start.rs:872` and again in `app/src-tauri/src/hostedsessions.rs:4951`. The admin and team refusal is in `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox.rs:8284`.
- **Possible gap: the floor's process rules exist only in the wrap.** `FLOOR_HELD` (no `launchctl`/`osascript`/`open`/`crontab`/`at`, no Apple Events) is in the Seatbelt wrap only. Claude Code at the floor runs on its own sandbox, and I found no equivalent exec deny in `claude.rs`. Whether Claude Code's own profile blocks these is not stated in the code, so check before treating it as a uniform rule.
- **No process, CPU or memory limits on chats on a person's own machine.** I found no `setrlimit` and no cgroup for chats. Only runner containers are bounded (`dockerpool.rs`, `poollimits.rs`). purlis raises the open-files soft limit for itself (`openfiles.rs`), and chats inherit it.
- **`SSH_AUTH_SOCK` is passed in the environment** (`chatenv.rs:70`), but a sandboxed chat reaches no unix socket without a grant. So under the sandbox the variable is passed but the agent behind it cannot be used, while an unsandboxed chat can use it.
- **Claude Code's shared `/tmp/claude` is writable by every sandboxed command** (Claude Code's own default, `claude.rs:112-119`). It is denied only for outcome-eval shells. Wraps give each chat its own temp instead.
- **Handbacks and wrapped harnesses.** For Codex and opencode the handbacks folder is readable from inside the sandbox, because their hooks run inside it (`sandbox.rs:1797` comment). Only a per-file check guards it until delivery moves to the hook socket (#1457).
- **The settings purlis writes for Claude Code** are all in `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/claude.rs:174` (`settings`). That covers `sandbox.filesystem.denyRead`/`denyWrite`/`allowWrite`, `network.allowUnixSockets`, `allowLocalBinding`, `allowedDomains`, `strictAllowlist` and `enableWeakerNetworkIsolation`, plus `permissions.deny` (`Read(...)`/`Edit(...)`, WebFetch, WebSearch, `mcp__claude-in-chrome`). Only the folder parts are in my tables; hosts, sockets, web tools and browser tools belong to the network and tools families.
- **Out of scope here.** `app/src-tauri/src/readonly.rs` (a project opened read-only by an older purlis) is a project-compatibility gate, not a chat decision; the core's `compat::read_only` is what refuses writes. `app/src-tauri/src/enginesandbox.rs` is about the browser engine's own sandbox (AppArmor on Ubuntu): purlis never starts the engine without it, and the person chooses between adding the profile and using this machine's Chrome. That belongs in the browser family.
- **Bounds that fail closed.** These are hard-coded, nobody can change them, and hitting one refuses the chat: `KeptUntold`, `Unread` (64 moves / 1024 names), `RepositoriesUnfound` (50k), `GitFolderUnheld`, `PLANE_FILE_MAX` (1 MiB) and `MOST_PROFILE` (768 KiB of Seatbelt profile, `seatbelt.rs:71`). Each is a candidate "limit" key, though making any of them configurable would weaken the sandbox.

### Network, ports, the browser

- **Harness coverage gap.** Only Claude Code, Codex and opencode can be sandboxed (`crates/purlis-core/src/harness.rs:107`). A sandboxed project refuses ACP chats (`crates/purlis-core/src/acp.rs:13`). An unsandboxed chat of any harness has the person's whole network and every loopback port. So every N/S/P row applies only while a sandbox is in force.
- **The floor and full levels differ a lot on the network.** At the floor (Basic, the person's default) every public host is open and nothing is asked (N2). Presets, hosts and live asks (N3 to N25) decide anything only at the full sandbox. A policy needs "level" as a condition on every host rule.
- **The port meaning of a host listed without a port differs by harness.** Claude Code carries it on any port (`decide_on_any_port`), and the wraps on 443/80 only (N29). The same `charter.toml` line grants different things on different harnesses.
- **Claude Code older than 2.1.285** keeps its own proxy (N35). For those chats purlis's reach decisions, live asks, local-address check and network record do not apply. Claude's own `allowedDomains` decides instead.
- **`allowLocalBinding`.** purlis always writes `false`, but an administrator's managed Claude settings outrank it. purlis only reports that in doctor (`crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/claude.rs:832`) and does not refuse. It is a policy key held outside purlis.
- **One admin lock, `socket-grants: false`, covers four things:** sockets and services, ports to connect to, ports to listen on, and the certificate check (`crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/policy.rs:709`-`:745`, `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox.rs:367`). A unified policy will probably want to split them.
- **Possible doc/code tension on Claude listen grants.** `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox.rs:7466` says "No door opens for a Claude Code chat" and withdraws its listen-only ports. `app/src-tauri/src/hooks.rs:2120` and `#3316` say a Claude Code chat on macOS serves through a dial-in door (slots over the hook socket, `CHAT_SLOTS_MOST`). It is probably consistent: the door is opened by the slot path, not by the compiled reach. Worth confirming.
- **Live state.** Allow next time, Keep blocked, Allow for this chat, and held asks are in memory only, and are lost on restart.
- **Out of scope here:** `crates/purlis-core/src/netlog.rs` logs purlis's own feature calls (30 days, `crates/purlis-core/src/netlog.rs:148`) and decides nothing about chats. The confined-extension proxy (`special_use`, `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/hosts.rs:405`) is for extensions, not chats.
- **Browser sub-inventory notes** (verified by a sub-agent):
  - `EngineSettings::run_outdated` (`crates/browser-engine/src/install.rs:479`) is documented as "the person allowed an outdated engine in Settings" (install.rs:37), but no code sets it: core builds `EngineSettings { engine, ..default() }` (`crates/purlis-core/src/browsers.rs:402`). The 21-day refusal is in effect hard-coded. Either the doc or the code is wrong.
  - Saving downloads (row 50) is always refused. The per-origin yes (`Files::allow_saves`) exists only under `#[cfg(test)]` until the save prompt (#3746) lands.
  - The pre-approval of browser tools (row 30) is written only into Claude Code's allow list. Codex and OpenCode have no equivalent rule. Whether those harnesses prompt for each `mcp__purlis__browser_*` call is up to the harness, so the effective "ask" is different on each harness.
  - Several decisions are held only in memory (posture, chat-level site Allows, fill and login grants, pool opt-ins, Keep-blocked counts). A relaunch resets them. A unified policy would have to decide whether these stay session-scoped.
  - Limit checks happen in two layers. The per-project count is checked in the app (chatbrowsers.rs, 4 call sites), and the device budget in the session host (hosting.rs:4618). A runner reads its own machine's budget.
  - In the policy shape, the levels for browser-site grants (chat / you / project) are the sandbox grant levels (`crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/grant.rs`). They are gated by the same `allow-scopes` lock as host grants, but stored apart: `app/src-tauri/src/sandbox.json` `browser_sites_mine`, and `[sandbox] browser-sites` in charter.toml.
  - `navguard.rs` (row 79) governs the Tauri window's webviews, not a chat's browser. It is listed because it was in scope.

### Guards

#### Notes: git


- No configurable protected-branch list exists. The only branch protection is #61 (no push to the remote's default branch) plus the forge's own rules (e.g. GitHub refusing unsigned commits, which #66 reacts to).
- Almost every git guard is a hard-coded mistake guard; the only project-file knob found in this area is `[plane] refuse_unsandboxed_commit` (#48). Sandbox on/off (person, project `[sandbox]`, admin locks) indirectly switches #21, #22, #40, #47.
- Fail directions differ for the same concern: claim check fails closed when brokered (#27) but open for plain git (#28); commitguard's allowlist check fails open after 3 s (#12); projectgit fails open where it cannot place a command, while the unsandboxed branch reader refuses (#24).
- Contradiction worth checking: gitbroker.rs:1271-1273 doc says "a commit the app makes for a chat is never signed (#1476)", but #67 (commitsign, #5480) now signs brokered commits after git makes them. The doc comment looks stale; the repo-level signer refusal (#57) still stands.
- Trailers: save strips app trailers (#45) while commit refuses them (#52): two answers to the same input.
- Git hooks arming (#14) has no off switch and silently does nothing off unix or when the hooks dir could not be written (commits then go unscanned; warning only in host log, crates/purlis-core/src/hoststart.rs:93).
- Adjacent, not covered here (other forks): the scan itself (`diffscan`, `secretshape`, `scanallow`), `toolgate`/`guardcaps` caps in front of every arm, `mergehold`, forge-side merge/close, `reposave`/`planesave`/`autosave`.

#### Notes: credential


- Nothing in this family has a project, person or admin-lock knob except the scan allowlist (A3/A4) and the forge host list feeding C6/C8. Everything else is HARD; a policy migration would need to decide whether any of it becomes a key at all (the code argues repeatedly that the leak arm must stay ungated).
- Harness coverage gap: the Read guard (R1-R3) is not armed for codex (`plugin.rs:127-134`: codex tools are not called `Read`/`Grep`), so on codex only the Bash leak arm guards vault reads. ACP harnesses: I found no route from an ACP permission request into `toolgate::verdict`/`pretooluse-read`; worth confirming by whoever owns the harness section.
- Leak guard is explicitly a mistake guard, "defeated by deliberate obfuscation" (`leakguard.rs:23-31`); the real boundary is the sandbox. Likewise the allowlist/hook arm (`scanallow.rs:22-26`).
- Overlap with other Guards rows: `githook.rs`, `githooks.rs`, `gitbroker`, `planegit`/`planesave`/`reposave`, `guardcaps`, `toolgate` are listed in the parent's scope; D1-D10 cite them because the scan decisions live there. Dedupe when merging.
- Plane-save push (D4 MULTI) refuses on credentials only, while a chat's own pre-push refuses personal data too: same scanner, different outcome per surface.
- Brokered commit (`gitbroker/commit.rs:1339`) checks the allowlist change and findings but not `stages_what_a_chat_runs_under`; that check lives separately in `brokered.rs:1375`. Not verified that both brokered paths apply it.

#### Notes: forge, merge, release


- The floor (rows merge 1, release 1-9, forge 21) lives in `floorguard.rs`, which the parent also assigned under "floor guard"; these rows are its merge/release/token decisions. Dedupe against the floor-guard table.
- "Attended/unattended" is the one knob for the floor, and it is not a setting: it comes from the chat's attendance fact (`attendance::of_a_line`); on Codex the harness reports `bypassPermissions` on every line, so only the held fact counts (toolgate.rs:357-362 doc). Candidate policy key: "publish/merge when unattended".
- Merge hold claims "no setting or key changes that" (mergehold.rs:17-19). All hold limits (3 asks, 1.5 s/1.2 s, 30 s watch, 1 MiB view, 2 presses) are hard-coded.
- `PRESSES` is only exposed via `Look`/looked.rs:117 to the window; the second press is enforced in the UI, not by the core's `press` (which merges on one call if the ask is `asked`). Not verified in app/src-tauri/src/src.
- Gap the code names itself: an unreadable merge (raw API, no number) is refused with no ask (mergehold.rs:44-46, "Not here yet").
- `docs/forge-tool-refusals.md` matches `args.rs`/`api.rs` as far as checked; the doc says `gh extension` is refused and glab's list in code also includes `extension` (doc's glab table does not list it).
- A chat's forge calls go through the person's `gh`/`glab` CLI login (route.rs:72-74): the guard rows here are therefore the only bound on what that login lets a chat do outside the hooks; there is no allowlist of forge verbs (issue close, label, issue edit, PR create all run). Only merge/release/token/credential verbs are guarded; prose in bodies is the prose guard's and leak guard's (proseguard.rs:208, leakguard.rs:859), not covered here.
- ACP harnesses: not verified whether their shell calls reach `guard pretooluse`; if not, none of the hook rows apply to them.
- Tag signing refusal (`git tag -s`, credguard.rs:487) belongs to the git/credential family.

#### Notes: save, kill switch, plumbing


- Nothing found that lets a policy or admin lock pin any save key (`[plane]`/`[repos]`) or the kill switch; the only knobs are the two TOML files and window presses. The repo's `.purlis`/admin "Policy" (CONTEXT.md:1950) does not reach this area today.
- `purlis guard allow` has no check on who runs it: a chat could run it and add an `allow` rule to the plane root's harness settings (P18/P19). Whether a sandbox or another guard (toolgate/plane-root arm) stops that was not traced here; worth checking in the tool gate / floor family.
- Kill switch residual (halt.rs:19-24, killswitch.rs:13): any process as the operator's user, an agent included, can edit `halted` and `kill-switch.jsonl`; a forged re-arm while the app is down is believed at next launch. Nothing stops a chat from running `purlis stop --all` (that only stops, never re-arms).
- `[plane] mode` unset means different things by caller: the app asks and auto-save never runs; `purlis save` (and the window's Save press for pushing, planegit.rs:4147) treat it as push. A single policy key should state which.
- P1: hook coverage for ACP agents and remote (vendor) chats was not confirmed; the registry is wired for claude, codex and opencode only. Codex has no command-pattern permission file, so P18 rules never reach it (purlis's own guard is all it gets).
- P15 is deliberately fail-open (no app answer lets git through), unlike P14 (merge hold, fail-closed); a unified policy should make that asymmetry explicit.
- S29's rule and its allow list are the credential family's (secretshape/scanallow); only the save-side wiring is listed here.

#### Notes: shell guard, tool gate, prose, floor, handoff


- `guardcaps.rs` (128 KiB, nesting 8, 64 layers, depth 16) was not assigned to this fork, but every arm here depends on it. It should get rows of its own in the Guards family if no other fork covers it.
- These tool-gate arms live outside the files assigned to the Guards family: `planeroot`, `rootupdate`, `cloneupdate`, `consentspelling`, `rulespelling`, `dispatchguard`, `projectgit`. They are given summary rows above (the deciding function and its lists). Read their full keyword lists (for example `planeroot.rs:125` `RESTORE_OPTS`, `:171` `GIT_KNOWN_SUBCOMMANDS`) if exhaustive keys are needed.
- Out of scope but part of the same PreToolUse surface: `personagate` (the allow-only persona tool gate, which runs after these refusals), and the `pretooluse-read`/`pretooluse-edit` file-tool guards in `toolhooks.rs` (hookreg `:96`, `:102`, 5 s timeouts).
- The "no override" rule (`toolgate.rs:75`) conflicts with a single project policy: these guards are built so that no file can lift them. A future policy key that relaxes any arm would contradict that ruling (charter#370) and needs an explicit decision.
- A10 applies to Claude Code only (`dispatchguard.rs:450`). Other harnesses get no rider refusal, because only Claude Code has the pre-allow it protects.
- A7b's consent patterns still spell `charter …` (`scaffold/settings.rs:44-61`), with `purlis …` twins at `:83` and `:89`; the retired handoff patterns are at `:99`.
- The "is this a sub-agent" judgement is made twice, by `handoffguard::Caller::from_a_subagent` (A7) and by `dispatchguard` (A7d). Both use the same list of measured harnesses.

### Asks and consents, the harness permission mode

- **Doc vs code (Codex).** `crates/purlis-core/src/attendance.rs:22-23` says "purlis sets its [Codex's] approval policy itself". But `crates/purlis-core/src/harness/codex.rs:209-211` says an unsandboxed Codex chat keeps Codex's own settings, and `sandbox::codex::flags()` (which sets `approval_policy`) is applied only on the sandboxed line (`crates/purlis-core/src/harness/codex.rs:502`). For unsandboxed Codex, purlis sets no approval policy.
- **Stale ADR table.** ADR 0121's amendment of 2026-11-10 ("Quiet is built…") says no Expiring ask is held open yet. But `crates/purlis-core/src/sandbox/asks.rs:59` (`MOST_HELD_OPEN`, 15 min) and `app/src-tauri/src/asking.rs:4649` implement hold-open for held connections.
- **The permission mode is not a purlis setting today.** It lives only in profile command flags and the person's own harness settings files. Nothing per chat or per project in purlis chooses it, except the task cap (Perm #6) and the grade pin (Perm #11). A policy key for "permission mode" would be new.
- **Codex and opencode tasks never get auto.** `task_mode_words` is implemented only for Claude (`crates/purlis-core/src/dispatchmode.rs:38-42` doc).
- **`Begun::Loop` exists but nothing uses it.** It is declared, but no start path names it yet (`crates/purlis-core/src/attendance.rs:67-69`).
- **Silence limits.** The admin policy ceiling covers every `Limit`, notes included (`crates/purlis-core/src/dispatchlimits.rs:1349`, `:160-170`). These rows overlap the dispatch family.
- **Rows that overlap other families.** Sandbox-block asks (Asks #30–35), dispatch grants (Asks #32, #60), the release floor and guards (Asks #58, Perm #29) and merges (Asks #46) are listed here only as asks. Their substance belongs to other families.
- **Not found / not verified.** `ask_operator` gets an hour on Codex (`CODEX_TOOL_TIMEOUT_SECS`). I found no purlis-side timeout for it on Claude, so Claude's own MCP timeout applies. The "Remind me" setting (Off/Gently/Often) and quiet hours live in the TS layout prefs (`app/src-tauri/src/src`), not in Rust, and I did not cite their lines.

### Dispatch

- `dispatchwaits.rs`, `dispatchliveness.rs`, `dispatch.rs` (legacy committed log), `handed.rs` decide nothing a chat may do: they describe (blocked-by counts, liveness, tallies) or are extension plumbing. Left out of the table.
- The protection of grant files and `[dispatch]` (#32) is the sandbox's: core docs say plainly (D-1437-R2) that a chat in a project with the sandbox off runs as the person and can write `[dispatch.grants]`, `app/src-tauri/src/sandbox.json` and `app/src-tauri/src/dispatch-never.json`. A single policy should state the tier of each of these stores.
- Sub-agent refusals (#2, #3, #106) are recognition, not a boundary, and nothing is refused on opencode (no measured `agent_id`). ACP harnesses are not named anywhere in this family: no pre-allow for dispatch tools (#100) and no sub-agent detection. Worth confirming that ACP chats are covered at all.
- The pre-allows (#97–#104) exist only for Claude Code. On other harnesses the harness's own prompt asks beside purlis's grant, so the outcome for one decision depends on the harness.
- Bypass detection (#41) recognises flags only. A wrapper script, or a harness setting kept in a file, is not seen (the code says so).
- `purlis.toml` vs `charter.toml`: the docs cite `charter.toml`, while the app tests use `purlis.toml`. Both are read as the manifest.
- The limits table holds five "notes" (#56, #57) that bound nothing a chat may do. They share the `[dispatch]` table and the ceiling machinery, so a policy key list should separate them from real limits.
- Runner-origin dispatches (#76) ignore grants entirely, and "moves work" (#74) also ignores grants. Both are hard-coded "ask the person" and nobody can change them.
- Harness for the persona gate (#111–#118): it is reached via `purlis guard` (Bash PreToolUse). I did not verify which harness adapters arm that hook beyond Claude Code.

### Other (pools, runners, retention, usage, git and worktree rules, hosted chats)

- Kill switch (row 41), merge hold (72), release floor (74), commit guard (56), clone/branch guards (57-58) and the risk class (77) are guard/ask machinery other parts probably also list; I kept them for the git/merge scope. Dedupe by Where.
- "Remote chats" (ADR 0076 §7, FD-19) exist only as the `observed` run kind in crates/purlis-core/src/state/run.rs:303/:637 (vendor-reportable states). Nothing lists or governs them yet, so they make no allow/deny decisions.
- "Hosted" in code means runner-hosted chats (`hostedchats.rs`); pooltools.rs says "a hosted one" is never sent a value. There is no separate cloud-hosted chat limit.
- usage.rs enforces no budget: there is no purlis token, cost or turn cap anywhere (grep for budget/max_turns/cost limit found none). Only Claude's statusline `rate_limits` and the `rate_limit` stop reason are read.
- The piece claim fails differently by route (row 66). A brokered write fails closed; the Bash guard on plain git fails open when no app answers (guard.rs:310, "any other answer, a No included, lets the git run"). A policy should pick one.
- `gitpolicy.rs` writes `commit.gpgsign=false` into every clone, but signing is otherwise supported (`signingsetup`, plane `[plane] sign`, push refused for unsigned commits waits on signing setup). These pull in different directions.
- Pool limits have two sources of truth, MULTI: `poollimits::ALL` holds the person-facing bounds and `dockerpool::Limits::new`/`SUSPEND_AFTER` hold the same numbers again, plus the host's `UNLINKED_FOR`. A cluster's limits come from the Pool CRD and are validated in both the operator and the desktop.
- Whether a chat on a runner may start without the sandbox is not decided in these files. It is the same start consent as a local chat (other part); here only its consequence (taint, row 17) is decided.
- `work.rs` covers work items (tracker links) and makes no allow/deny decisions on what a chat does, so it has no rows.

The counts are of table rows, not of distinct keys, because some rows overlap. The guards
restate the floor guard in their release, merge and forge tables. Other (B) rows 41, 56-58, 72,
74 and 77 repeat guard and dispatch rows. Dedupe by the Where column when building keys.
